This skill is a router: it tells you which tool to use for each file type (pdf, docx, xlsx, csv, json, images, archives, ebooks) so you read the right amount the right way instead of blindly running cat on a binary. Triggers: any mention of /mnt/user-data/uploads/, an uploaded_files section, a file_path tag, or a user asking about an uploaded file you have not yet read. Do NOT use this skill if the file content is already visible in your context inside a documents block \u2014 you already have it.\"\n     4\tcompatibility: \"claude.ai, Claude Desktop, Cowork \u2014 any surface where uploads land at /mnt/user-data/uploads/\"\n     5\tlicense: Proprietary. LICENSE.txt has complete terms\n     6\t---\n     7\t\n     8\t# Reading Uploaded Files\n     9\t\n    10\t## Why this skill exists\n    11\t\n    12\tWhen a user uploads a file in claude.ai, Claude Desktop, or Cowork,\n    13\tthe file is written to `/mnt/user-data/uploads/<filename>` and you are told the path\n    14\tin an `<uploaded_files>` block. **The content is not in your context.**\n    15\tYou must go read it.\n    16\t\n    17\tThe naive thing \u2014 `cat /mnt/user-data/uploads/whatever` \u2014 is wrong for\n    18\tmost files:\n    19\t\n    20\t- On a PDF it prints binary garbage.\n    21\t- On a 100MB CSV it floods your context with rows you will never use.\n    22\t- On a DOCX it prints the raw ZIP bytes.\n    23\t- On an image it does nothing useful at all.\n    24\t\n    25\tThis skill tells you the right first move for each type, and when to\n    26\thand off to a deeper skill.\n    27\t\n    28\t## General protocol\n    29\t\n    30\t1. **Look at the extension.** That is your dispatch key.\n    31\t2. **Stat before you read.** Large files need sampling, not slurping.\n    32\t   ```bash\n    33\t   stat -c '%s bytes, %y' /mnt/user-data/uploads/report.pdf\n    34\t   file /mnt/user-data/uploads/report.pdf\n    35\t   ```\n    36\t3. **Read just enough to answer the user's question.** If they asked\n    37\t   \"how many rows are in this CSV\", don't load the whole thing into\n    38\t   pandas \u2014 `wc -l` gives a fast approximation (it counts newlines,\n    39\t   not CSV records, so it may over-count if quoted fields contain\n    40\t   embedded newlines).\n    41\t4. **If a dedicated skill exists, go read it.** The table below tells\n    42\t   you when. The dedicated skills cover editing, creating, and advanced\n    43\t   operations that this skill does not.\n    44\t\n    45\t## `extract-text`\n    46\t\n    47\tFor docx, odt, epub, xlsx, pptx, rtf, and ipynb the first move is\n    48\t`extract-text <file>`. It emits markdown for docx/odt/epub (headings,\n    49\tbold, lists, links, tables), tab-separated rows under `## Sheet:`\n    50\theaders for xlsx, text under `## Slide N` headers for pptx, fenced\n    51\tcode cells for ipynb, and plain text for rtf. Pass `--format <fmt>`\n    52\twhen the extension is wrong or absent (e.g., `--format xlsx` on an\n    53\t`.xlsm`). If it errors on a file, `pandoc <file> -t plain` is a\n    54\tfallback; for xlsx/pptx, fall back to the dedicated skill's\n    55\tPython-based approach (openpyxl / python-pptx).\n    56\t\n    57\t## Dispatch table\n    58\t\n    59\t| Extension                         | First move                                           | Dedicated skill                           |\n    60\t| contents, do **not** auto-extract               | \u2014 (below)                                 |\n    74\t| `.gz` (single file)               | `zcat \\| head` \u2014 no manifest to list                 | \u2014 (below)                                 |\n    75\t| `.epub`, `.odt`                   | `extract-text`                                       | \u2014 (below)                                 |\n    76\t| `.rtf`                            | `extract-text`                                       | \u2014 (below)                                 |\n    77\t| `.ipynb`                          | `extract-text`                                       | \u2014 (below)                                 |\n    78\t| `.txt`, `.md`, `.log`, code files | `wc -c` then `head` or full `cat`                    | \u2014 (below)                                 |\n    79\t| Unknown                           | `file` then decide                                   | \u2014                                         |\n    80\t\n    81\t---\n    82\t\n    83\t## PDF\n    84\t\n    85\t**Never** `cat` a PDF \u2014 it prints binary garbage.\n    86\t\n    87\tQuick first move \u2014 get the page count and check if text is extractable:\n    88\t\n    89\t```bash\n    90\tpdfinfo /mnt/user-data/uploads/report.pdf\n    91\tpdftotext -f 1 -l 1 /mnt/user-data/uploads/report.pdf - | head -20\n    92\t```\n    93\t\n    94\tThen peek at the text content:\n    95\t\n    96\t```python\n    97\tfrom pypdf import PdfReader\n    98\tr = PdfReader(\"/mnt/user-data/uploads/report.pdf\")\n    99\tprint(f\"{len(r.pages)} pages\")\n   100\tprint(r.pages[0].extract_text()[:2000])\n   101\t```\n   102\t\n   103\tFor anything beyond a quick peek \u2014 figures, tables, attachments,\n   104\tforms, scanned PDFs, visual inspection, or choosing a reading strategy\n   105\t\u2014 go read `/mnt/skills/public/pdf-reading/SKILL.md`. It covers\n   106\tcontent inventory, text extraction vs. page rasterization, embedded\n   107\tcontent extraction, and document-type-aware reading strategies.\n   108\t\n   109\tFor PDF form filling, creation, merging, splitting, or watermarking,\n   110\tgo read `/mnt/skills/public/pdf/SKILL.md`.\n   111\t\n   112\t---\n   113\t\n   114\t## DOCX / DOC\n   115\t\n   116\tThe `docx` skill covers editing, creating, tracked changes, images.\n   117\tRead it if you need any of those. For a quick look:\n   118\t\n   119\t```bash\n   120\textract-text /mnt/user-data/uploads/memo.docx | head -200\n   121\t```\n   122\t\n   123\tLegacy `.doc` (not `.docx`) must be converted first \u2014 see the `docx`\n   124\tskill.\n   125\t\n   126\t---\n   127\t\n   128\t## XLSX / XLS / spreadsheets\n   129\t\n   130\tThe `xlsx` skill covers formulas, formatting, charts, creating. Read\n   131\tit if you need any of those. For a quick look at an `.xlsx`:\n   132\t\n   133\t```bash\n   134\textract-text /mnt/user-data/uploads/data.xlsx | head -100\n   135\t```\n   136\t\n   137\tFor `.xlsm`, add `--format xlsx` (same zip structure; only the\n   138\textension differs). When you need a structured preview in Python:\n   139\t\n   140\t```python\n   141\tfrom openpyxl import load_workbook\n   142\twb = load_workbook(\"/mnt/user-data/uploads/data.xlsx\", read_only=True)\n   143\tprint(\"Sheets:\", wb.sheetnames)\n   144\tws = wb.active\n   145\tfor row in ws.iter_rows(max_row=5, values_only=True):\n   146\t    print(row)\n   147\t```\n   148\t\n   149\t`read_only=True` matters \u2014 without it, openpyxl loads the entire\n   150\tworkbook into memory, which breaks on large files. Do not trust\n   151\t`ws.max_row` in read-only mode: many non-Excel writers omit the\n   152\tdimension record, so it comes back `None` or wrong. If you need a row\n   153\tcount, iterate or use pandas.\n   154\t\n   155\t**Legacy `.xls`** \u2014 openpyxl raises `InvalidFileException`. Use:\n   156\t\n   157\t```python\n   158\timport pandas as pd\n   159\tdf = pd.read_excel(\"/mnt/user-data/uploads/old.xls\", engine=\"xlrd\", nrows=5)\n   160\t```\n   161\t\n   162\t**`.ods` (OpenDocument)** \u2014 openpyxl also rejects this. Use:\n   163\t\n   164\t```python\n   165\timport pandas as pd\n   166\tdf = pd.read_excel(\"/mnt/user-data/uploads/data.ods\", engine=\"odf\", nrows=5)\n   167\t```\n   168\t\n   169\t---\n   170\t\n   171\t## PPTX\n   172\t\n   173\t```bash\n   174\textract-text /mnt/user-data/uploads/deck.pptx | head -200\n   175\t```\n   176\t\n   177\t**Legacy `.ppt`** \u2014 convert to `.pptx` first via LibreOffice; see\n   178\t`/mnt/skills/public/pptx/SKILL.md` for the sandbox-safe\n   179\t`scripts/office/soffice.py` wrapper (bare `soffice` hangs here because\n   180\tthe seccomp filter blocks the `AF_UNIX` sockets LibreOffice uses for\n   181\tinstance management).\n   182\t\n   183\tFor anything beyond reading, go to `/mnt/skills/public/pptx/SKILL.md`.\n   184\t\n   185\t---\n   186\t\n   187\t## CSV / TSV\n   188\t\n   189\t**Do not** `cat` or `head` these blindly. A CSV with a 50KB quoted cell\n   190\tin row 1 will wreck your `head -5`. Use pandas with `nrows`:\n   191\t\n   192\t```python\n   193\timport pandas as pd\n   194\tdf = pd.read_csv(\"/mnt/user-data/uploads/data.csv\", nrows=5)\n   195\tprint(df)\n   196\tprint()\n   197\tprint(df.dtypes)\n   198\t```\n   199\t\n   200\tApproximate row count without loading (over-counts if the file has\n   201\tRFC-4180 quoted newlines \u2014 the same quoted-cell case this section\n   202\twarned about above):\n   203\t\n   204\t```bash\n   205\twc -l /mnt/user-data/uploads/data.csv\n   206\t```\n   207\t\n   208\tFull analysis only after you know the shape:\n   209\t\n   210\t```python\n   211\tdf = pd.read_csv(\"/mnt/user-data/uploads/data.csv\")\n   212\tprint(df.describe())\n   213\t```\n   214\t\n   215\tTSV: same, with `sep=\"\\t\"`.\n   216\t\n   217\t---\n   218\t\n   219\t## JSON / JSONL\n   220\t\n   221\tStructure first, content second:\n   222\t\n   223\t```bash\n   224\tjq 'type' /mnt/user-data/uploads/data.json\n   225\tjq 'if type == \"array\" then length elif type == \"object\" then keys else . end' /mnt/user-data/uploads/data.json\n   226\t```\n   227\t\n   228\t(`keys` errors on scalar JSON roots \u2014 a bare `\"hello\"` or `42` is valid\n   229\tJSON per RFC 7159 \u2014 so guard the branch.)\n   230\t\n   231\tThen drill into what the user actually asked about.\n   232\t\n   233\tJSONL (one object per line) \u2014 do **not** `jq` the whole file; work line\n   234\tby line:\n   235\t\n   236\t```bash\n   237\thead -3 /mnt/user-data/uploads/data.jsonl | jq .\n   238\twc -l /mnt/user-data/uploads/data.jsonl\n   239\t```\n   240\t\n   241\t---\n   242\t\n   243\t## Images (JPG / PNG / GIF / WEBP)\n   244\t\n   245\t**You can already see uploaded images.** They are injected into your\n   246\tcontext as vision inputs alongside the `<uploaded_files>` pointer. You\n   247\tdo not need to read them from disk to describe them.\n   248\t\n   249\tThe disk copy is only needed if you are going to **process** the image\n   250\tprogrammatically:\n   251\t\n   252\t```python\n   253\tfrom PIL import Image\n   254\timg = Image.open(\"/mnt/user-data/uploads/photo.jpg\")\n   255\tprint(img.size, img.mode, img.format)\n   256\t```\n   257\t\n   258\tFor OCR on an image (text extraction, not description):\n   259\t\n   260\t```python\n   261\timport pytesseract\n   262\tprint(pytesseract.image_to_string(img))\n   263\t```\n   264\t\n   265\tNote: the client resizes images larger than 2000\u00d72000 down to that\n   266\tbound and re-encodes as JPEG before upload, so the disk copy may not\n   267\tbe the user's original bytes. For most processing this doesn't matter;\n   268\tif the user is asking about original-resolution pixel data, flag it.\n   269\t\n   270\t---\n   271\t\n   272\t## Archives (ZIP / TAR / TAR.GZ)\n   273\t\n   274\t**List first. Extract never \u2014 unless the user explicitly asks.**\n   275\tArchives can be huge, contain path traversal, or nest forever.\n   276\t\n   277\t```bash\n   278\tunzip -l /mnt/user-data/uploads/bundle.zip\n   279\ttar -tf /mnt/user-data/uploads/bundle.tar\n   280\t```\n   281\t\n   282\tGNU tar auto-detects compression \u2014 `tar -tf` works on `.tar`,\n   283\t`.tar.gz`, `.tar.bz2`, `.tar.xz` alike. Don't hard-code `-z`.\n   284\t\n   285\tIf the user wants one file from inside, extract just that one:\n   286\t\n   287\t```bash\n   288\tunzip -p /mnt/user-data/uploads/bundle.zip path/inside/file.txt\n   289\t```\n   290\t\n   291\t**Standalone `.gz`** (not a tar) compresses a single file \u2014 there is\n   292\tno manifest to list. Just peek at the decompressed content:\n   293\t\n   294\t```bash\n   295\tzcat /mnt/user-data/uploads/data.json.gz | head -50\n   296\t```\n   297\t\n   298\t---\n   299\t\n   300\t## EPUB / ODT\n   301\t\n   302\t```bash\n   303\textract-text /mnt/user-data/uploads/book.epub | head -200\n   304\t```\n   305\t\n   306\tFor long ebooks, pipe through `head` \u2014 you rarely need the whole thing\n   307\tto answer a question.\n   308\t\n   309\t---\n   310\t\n   311\t## RTF / IPYNB\n   312\t\n   313\t```bash\n   314\textract-text /mnt/user-data/uploads/notes.rtf | head -200\n   315\textract-text /mnt/user-data/uploads/notebook.ipynb | head -200\n   316\t```\n   317\t\n   318\t---\n   319\t\n   320\t## Plain text / code / logs\n   321\t\n   322\tCheck the size first:\n   323\t\n   324\t```bash\n   325\twc -c /mnt/user-data/uploads/app.log\n   326\t```\n   327\t\n   328\t- **Under ~20KB**: `cat` is fine.\n   329\t- **Over ~20KB**: `head -100` and `tail -100` to orient. If the user\n   330\t  asked about something specific, `grep` for it. Load the whole thing\n   331\t  only if you genuinely need all of it.\n   332\t\n   333\tFor log files, the user almost always cares about the end:\n   334\t\n   335\t```bash\n   336\ttail -200 /mnt/user-data/uploads/app.log\n   337\t```\n   338\t\n   339\t---\n   340\t\n   341\t## Unknown extension\n   342\t\n   343\t```bash\n   344\tfile /mnt/user-data/uploads/mystery.bin\n   345\txxd /mnt/user-data/uploads/mystery.bin | head -5\n   346\t```\n   347\t\n   348\t`file` identifies most things. `xxd` head shows magic bytes. Each new edge case adds another conditional rather than refactoring the underlying logic.\n    62\t\n    63\tExample: `if x: if y: if z: ... if w: ...` chains that nobody can audit.\n    64\t\n    65\tCounter: **before adding a new rule, check if the existing rules can be simplified**. The race is still there.\n\nCounter: **always trace the failure to its root cause** before patching. If you don't have time to fix the root, log a backlog item explicitly naming the bandage.\n\n### Pattern B \u2014 Building without wiring\n\nSymptom: writing a module that compiles and tests in isolation but doesn't integrate with anything else.\n\nExample: building S6 with mocked-out S5 calls, never verifying the real S5 contract.\n\nCounter: **integration test before celebration**. Every new module must demonstrate it talks to its real upstream/downstream peers correctly.\n\n### Pattern C \u2014 Scores without truth\n\nSymptom: introducing metrics (\"liveability score 72/100\") that aren't traced to specific user-observable outcomes.\n\nExample: a \"code quality\" score that doesn't predict real bugs.\n\nCounter: **every metric must be tied to a user-visible outcome** with documented derivation. Otherwise it's a vanity score and may actively mislead.\n\n### Pattern D \u2014 Rules on rules\n\nSymptom: stacking constraint layers without simplifying. Each new edge case adds another conditional rather than refactoring the underlying logic.\n\nExample: `if x: if y: if z: ... if w: ...` chains that nobody can audit.\n\nCounter: **before adding a new rule, check if the existing rules can be simplified**. Sometimes the right move is to refactor backward, not patch forward.\n\n### Pattern E \u2014 Scope creep mid-build (the most expensive)\n\nSymptom: while implementing a locked spec, you decide to also fix a related issue you just noticed. The unscoped work bleeds into commits, tests, and reviews.\n\nExample: while building S6, deciding to also refactor S3's option_generator because you noticed inefficiency.\n\nCounter: **the locked spec is the only scope**. Any unrelated finding goes to the backlog, NOT into the current build. Defer ruthlessly.\n\n---\n\n## WHEN APPLYING THESE\n\nWhen Ramalingam (or his critique source) flags an issue:\n\n1. PATCH CODE\n   \u2193\n   Code-critique returns N drawbacks\n   Claude analyses each, patches what's valid, pushes back (D-067) where appropriate\n   Re-runs tests\n   Delivers patched code\n\n   \u2193\n\n   MOVE TO NEXT BUILD (S{N+1})\n   Loop back to step 1\n```\n\n---\n\n## CONTINUATION DISCIPLINE\n\n- **Continue building until Ramalingam says stop.**\n- The decision to stop is **HIS**, not Claude's.\n- Build cycles do NOT end at session boundaries.\n- Build cycles do NOT end because the conversation is \"long.\"\n\n## CLAUDE'S RESPONSIBILITIES\n\n- \u2705 Suggest the next sensible step within the cycle\n- \u2705 Be honest about what's done and what's pending\n- \u2705 Apply D-067 pushback when critique would force YAGNI/Pattern E surgery\n- \u274c Do NOT suggest handoff\n- \u274c Do NOT mention context budget casually\n- \u274c Do NOT use context as a stop-prompting signal\n\n## THE ONE EXCEPTION (CONTEXT QUALITY)\n\nIf at any point during building Claude judges that **context is genuinely too low to complete the current step at the quality level of prior chats** \u2014 Claude tells Ramalingam straight, ONCE:\n\n> \"Context is now insufficient to do S{N} code/spec at the quality of this chat \u2014 you should hand off after this step.\"\n\nThis is a quality-protection signal, not a stop-prompting signal. Claude is honest about it once when it matters; doesn't repeat it.\n\n## HANDOFF TRIGGER\n\nHandoff happens **only when Ramalingam explicitly says \"now hand off\"** or equivalent.\n\nWhen that happens, Claude produces (per **D-068 principle + D-069 format**):\n\n**Format:** ONE single zip file (`handoff_S{N}.zip` or similar).\n\n**Contents (chronologically organized):**\n1. `00_START_HERE/` \u2014 orientation files (NEXT_CLAUDE_HANDOFF.md, obligations, build cycle rule)\n2. `01_master_doc/` \u2014 full master doc updated with everything done across all conversations (chronological)\n3. `02_specs_chronological/` \u2014 every spec file (drafts + locked) in chronological order\n4. `03_code_chronological/` \u2014 every code file (built + patched) organized by session\n5. `04_backlog/` \u2014 full backlog\n6. `05_integrity_check/` \u2014 file-by-file inventory + verification + checksums\n\n**NOT a giant consolidated .md** \u2014 exceeds download limits.\n**NOT a folder of loose files** \u2014 cumbersome to pass around.\n**ONE zip.** User extracts and reads at leisure.\n\nThe zip is the single canonical deliverable. Pass the zip to the next Claude or extract it yourself; either works.\n\n---\n\n## D-067 \u2014 CRITIQUE PUSHBACK RULE (extension)\n\nWhen the latest critique round would force:\n- **(a)** Pattern E surgery for hypothetical future need\n- **(b)** Optimisation for cases that don't currently exist\n- **(c)** Overcorrection with hostile mechanisms (caller-frame inspection in tests, graceful degradation that masks invariant violations, rule engines for trivial named predicates)\n\nClaude **pushes back transparently**, citing prior decisions (D-065) and YAGNI principles. Pushback is documented in the response so Ramalingam can override if he disagrees.\n\nExamples already on record (from B-027 code-critique round 2):\n- D3 (caller-frame inspection enforcement) \u2014 REJECTED: hostile to test fixtures\n- D5 (graceful degradation on missing dispatch) \u2014 REJECTED: would mask programmer bugs\n- D7 (rule engine for 3 named predicates) \u2014 REJECTED: YAGNI\n\nThese three were rejected and logged transparently in the backlog so the decision is visible.\n\n---\n\nThe chain holds.\n",

        "text": "   582\t================================================================\n   583\t\u00a7 2. INVARIANTS (NON-NEGOTIABLE)\n   584\t================================================================\n   585\t\n   586\tS8 introduces six new invariants (P36\u2013P41) that extend the S7b\n   587\tchain. None modify S7b's P19\u2013P35.\n   588\t\n   589\t\u00a7 2.1  P36 \u2014 Validation tests are deterministic (target).\n   590\t\n   591\t       Every test in `tests/validation/` and `tests/e2e/` aims\n   592\t       to produce the same result on every run, in every order,\n   593\t       on every machine running Python 3.10+ with the project's\n   594\t       pinned deps. **\"Zero flakes\" is the aspiration; <0.5%\n   595\t       flake rate measured over 100+ runs is the operational\n   596\t       target.** This reflects industry norms \u2014 Slack, GitHub,\n   597\t       and Atlassian (per TestDino benchmarks) target <2% with\n   598\t       retry-once policy; <0.5% is more strict and achievable\n   599\t       given S8's hermetic design.\n   600\t\n   601\t       Mechanism:\n   602\t       - Time is controlled via a `FrozenClock` fixture that\n   603\t         makes time.time() and int(time.time()) return\n   604\t         deterministic values.\n   605\t       - Random-token generators are seeded for the test session.\n   606\t       - SQLite uses `:memory:` or `tempfile` (no shared state\n   607\t         between tests).\n   608\t       - HTTP responses don't depend on real network (Resend\n   609\t         hook is mocked via `unittest.mock.patch` per S7b \u00a7 8.6).\n   610\t       - E2E tests use only Playwright `page.get_by_*` semantic\n   611\t         locators (which auto-wait per Playwright docs), NEVER\n   612\t         `waitForTimeout()` (Playwright docs explicitly call this\n   613\t         out as \"inherently flaky\"). Web-verified at multiple\n   614\t         sources (TestDino, BrowserStack, BetterStack, Semaphore).\n   615\t       - `C3A_TEST_MODE=1` env var disables the auto-retry logic\n   616\t         in _shared.js c3aFetch (External Item 3) \u2014 auto-retry\n   617\t         timing is itself a flake source.\n   618\t\n   619\t       Quarantine policy (per Slack / Atlassian pattern):\n   620\t       - A test that flakes \u22653 times in CI within 1 sprint is\n   621\t         tagged `@pytest.mark.flaky_quarantine` and assigned an\n   622\t         owner with a 1-sprint deadline to fix or delete.\n   623\t       - CI metrics track flake rate per test; >0.5% rate\n   624\t         triggers quarantine review.\n   625\t       - A retry-once policy is allowed in CI to prevent flakes\n   626\t         from blocking unrelated PRs; the retry is recorded so\n   627\t         flake rate metrics see it.\n   628\t\n   629\t       Verification: re-run the suite 10+ times in random order\n   630\t       during pre-merge testing; 0 flakes locally is the bar.\n   631\t       In CI: <0.5% over rolling 100 runs.\n   632\t\n   633\t\u00a7 2.2  P37 \u2014 Validation tests run in tiered time budgets.\n   634\t\n   635\t       Two tiers with separate hard caps (External Item 4):\n   636\t\n   637\t       **Tier 1 \u2014 `tests/validation/`** (in-process API tests):\n   638\t       - **30-second hard cap** on a developer laptop.\n   639\t       - Runs ALWAYS \u2014 every PR, every CI run, every local\n   640\t         `pytest`.\n   641\t       - Soft warning at 25s with **enforcement teeth (post-LOCK\n   642\t         PL2):** if Tier 1 exceeds 25s on **3 consecutive CI\n   643\t         runs**, this triggers a test-budget-review per the\n   644\t         hygiene rule in \u00a7 4.0.1 (audit recent test additions\n   645\t         for invariant-justification, consider pruning or\n   646\t         moving to Tier 2). Single-run >25s is a soft warning\n   647\t         only; sustained >25s is the trigger. CI implementation:\n   648\t         CI metric `c3a.tier1.duration_secs` tracked; alert\n   649\t         fires when 3 most-recent runs all >25s. Without this\n   650\t         enforcement teeth, the v0.3 \"soft warning\" was purely\n   651\t         advisory and would erode silently as tests accumulate.\n   652\t\n   653\t       **Tier 2 \u2014 `tests/e2e/`** (browser-driven Playwright tests):\n   654\t       - **60-second hard cap** on a developer laptop (includes\n   655\t         browser warmup ~5s).\n   656\t       - Tagged `@pytest.mark.e2e` \u2014 gated, NOT run by default\n   657\t         on PR pre-merge runs.\n   658\t       - Run on nightly CI + on pre-deploy verification runs.\n   659\t       - Locally invoked: `pytest -m e2e` or\n   660\t         `pytest tests/e2e/`.\n   661\t\n   662\t       **Combined cap when both run: 90 seconds.** Headroom\n   663\t       built into each tier independently so one tier blowing\n   664\t       its budget doesn't take down the other.\n   665\t\n   666\t       Current Tier 1 budget allocation post-PL1 + P-1\n   667\t       (concurrency 50\u219225, observability moved to Tier-2):\n   668\t       - state_machine ~5s (incl. 3 raw cross-check tests)\n   669\t       - idempotency ~3s\n   670\t       - termination ~3s\n   671\t       - 24h_fallback ~5s\n   672\t       - admin_recovery ~2s\n   673\t       - failure_modes ~2s\n   674\t       - concurrency ~2.6s (75 looped + 3 deterministic\n   675\t         worst-case anchor tests; v1.2 P-3)\n   676\t       - status ~1s\n   677\t       - validation fixture init/teardown overhead ~3s\n   678\t       - Total: ~26.6s; effective slack ~3.4s before 30s cap.\n   679\t       - **Target: ~27s steady-state with 3s buffer** (v1.2 P-1\n   680\t         hardening \u2014 was ~29.5s with ~0.5s slack in v1.1).\n   681\t\n   682\t       Tier 2 budget allocation (post-P-1 promotion +\n   683\t       v1.2 P-4 flow controller test):\n   684\t       - browser warmup (chromium launch) ~5s\n   685\t       - 5 browser flow tests \u00d7 ~7s ~35s\n   686\t       - 5 a11y tests \u00d7 ~3s ~15s\n   687\t       - observability tests promoted v1.2 P-1 (~6 tests) ~3s\n   688\t       - flow controller tests v1.2 P-4 (~3 tests) ~2s\n   689\t       - fixture overhead ~5s\n   690\t       - Total: ~65s; over the 60s soft cap.\n   691\t       - **Tier-2 cap raised v1.2: 75s** (was 60s) to\n   692\t         accommodate P-1 + P-4 additions. Acceptable because\n   693\t         Tier-2 only runs nightly + pre-deploy, never on PR\n   694\t         critical path.\n   695\t\n   696\t       **Tight-margin acknowledgment (v1.1 Item 2 + v1.2 P-1):**\n   697\t       v1.1's ~29.5s/30s budget was uncomfortably tight by\n   698\t       design. v1.2 P-1 hardens this by moving observability\n   699\t       (~3s; 6 tests, integration-test territory not core\n   700\t       correctness) from Tier 1 to Tier 2. New Tier 1 target\n   701\t       ~27s steady-state with 3s buffer. The previous safety\n   702\t       net remains:\n   703\t       (1) PL2 enforcement (\u00a7 2.2 above) \u2014 3-consecutive-runs\n   704\t           >25s triggers test-budget-review.\n   705\t       (2) \u00a7 4.0.1 hygiene rule \u2014 every new test justifies\n   706\t           an invariant; consolidation review on overlap.\n   707\t       (3) B-055 backlog item \u2014 captures post-launch tuning\n   708\t           trigger if real CI metrics show pressure.\n   709\t       Code build (Session 27+) MUST use the per-test budget\n   710\t       above as a hard constraint when implementing; if any\n   711\t       test exceeds its allocated slice during build, that\n   712\t       triggers review BEFORE adding the test, not after.\n   713\t\n   714\t       **PUSHBACK on prescriptive budget (v1.2 Item 1):** the\n   715\t       round-3 critic's \"Tier-1 must target ~24-26s\" rule is\n   716\t       prescription without CI variance data. Our actual CI\n   717\t       variance is unknown until S8 code ships. ~27s with 3s\n   718\t       buffer is more honest target \u2014 gives PL2 headroom AND\n   719\t       acknowledges the real budget rather than chasing a\n   720\t       lower number for its own sake. Post-launch B-055 trigger\n   721\t       will retune if real data shows the buffer is insufficient.\n   722\t\n   723\t       PR/CI gating:\n   724\t       - PR run: `pytest -m \"not e2e\"` (Tier 1 only \u2014 fast\n   725\t         feedback, gates merge)\n   726\t       - Nightly run: `pytest` (both tiers)\n   727\t       - Pre-deploy: `pytest` + `smoke_c3a_deploy.py` (full\n   728\t         in-process + post-deploy)\n   729\t\n   730\t       If P37 is at risk (3-consecutive-runs trigger fires),\n   731\t       address by:\n   732\t       - Sharing a single in-process HTTP server across tests\n   733\t         in the same module (setUpClass / setUpModule / pytest\n   734\t         fixture session scope).\n   735\t       - Using `:memory:` SQLite where the test doesn't need\n   736\t         persistence across in-process actors.\n   737\t       - Moving lower-priority tests from Tier 1 to Tier 2 via\n   738\t         `@pytest.mark.e2e` retag.\n   739\t       - Reusing the playwright browser context across e2e tests\n   740\t         in the same module.\n   741\t       - NOT by parallelizing test execution. Determinism (P36)\n   742\t         > speed.\n   743\t\n   744\t\u00a7 2.3  P38 \u2014 Validation tests are HERMETIC.\n   745\t\n   746\t       Test execution does not require:\n   747\t       - Network access\n   748\t       - A running Resend account\n   749\t       - A specific machine clock value\n   750\t       - Environment variables beyond what the test sets itself\n   751\t         (in particular: NO RESEND_API_KEY required; NO\n   752\t         BUILDEMUP_DATABASE_PATH required)\n   753\t       - File-system locations outside tempfile.gettempdir()\n   754\t\n   755\t       Playwright e2e tests require the chromium binary\n   756\t       (one-time `playwright install chromium` per dev machine\n   757\t       and per CI runner) but the test execution itself does not\n   758\t       need network beyond localhost.\n   759\t\n   760\t       Hermetic = reproducible = can run anywhere.\n   761\t\n   762\t\u00a7 2.4  P39 \u2014 Validation tests document the C3a contract.\n   763\t\n   764\t       Each test class has a docstring naming the invariant or\n   765\t       behavior under test in spec terms. A reader who finds a\n   766\t       test failure can identify which P-invariant or which \u00a7\n   767\t       section is being exercised without reading the test body.\n   768\t\n   769\t       Operational benefit: when production behavior drifts from\n   770\t       spec, the failing test names which contract is being\n   771\t       violated.\n   772\t\n   773\t\u00a7 2.5  P40 \u2014 UI tests exercise UI through a real browser engine.\n   774\t\n   775\t       E2E tests use Playwright + chromium against a live\n   776\t       in-process server. They do NOT use jsdom, JSDOMs, or any\n   777\t       DOM-mock library. The browser engine catches real-world\n   778\t       issues (layout, focus management, form submission encoding,\n   779\t       redirect handling) that mocks cannot.\n   780\t\n   781\t       Cost: one-time `playwright install chromium` per\n   782\t       dev/CI machine. Worth it for the realism guarantee.\n   783\t\n   784\t\u00a7 2.6  P41 \u2014 UI tests use semantic selectors.\n   785\t\n   786\t       Selectors prefer (in order):\n   787\t       (a) ARIA role + accessible name: `page.get_by_role(\"button\",\n   788\t           name=\"Confirm\")`\n   789\t       (b) Form label: `page.get_by_label(\"Email\")`\n   790\t       (c) Visible text: `page.get_by_text(\"Resolve case\")`\n   791\t       (d) test-id attributes (data-testid=\"...\") for genuinely\n   792\t           unique non-textual elements.\n   793\t\n   794\t       Selectors do NOT use:\n   795\t       - Brittle CSS path selectors (`.container > .card:nth-child(2)`)\n   796\t       - Auto-generated class names\n   797\t       - XPath in any form\n   798\t\n   799\t       Tests survive cosmetic CSS changes by depending on\n   800\t       semantics, not layout.\n   801\t\n   802\t\u00a7 2.7  P42 \u2014 UI\u2013API contract is centralized (External Item 1).\n   803\t\n   804\t       All response-shape assertions live in\n   805\t       `tests/validation/_contract.py` as plain Python functions:\n   806\t\n   807\t       ```python\n   808\t       def assert_check_response(resp: dict) -> None:\n   809\t           \"\"\"Asserts /api/extreme-case/check 200 response shape.\"\"\"\n   810\t           assert \"session_token\" in resp\n   811\t           assert isinstance(resp[\"session_token\"], str)\n   812\t           assert resp.get(\"ok\") is True\n   813\t           assert \"trace_id\" in resp\n   814\t           assert _TRACE_ID_RE.match(resp[\"trace_id\"])\n   815\t           # ... full shape check including extreme_case nested fields\n   816\t       ```\n   817\t\n   818\t       One assertion function per endpoint:\n   819\t       - assert_check_response()\n   820\t       - assert_resolve_response()\n   821\t       - assert_abort_response()\n   822\t       - assert_cba_verified_response()\n   823\t       - assert_status_response()       (new in v0.2)\n   824\t       - assert_validation_error()       (4xx shape)\n   825\t       - assert_storage_busy_error()     (503 shape)\n   826\t\n   827\t       Both validation tests AND e2e tests use these \u2014 UI tests\n   828\t       can call assert_check_response() against the JSON they\n   829\t       observe in the browser network tab (via Playwright's\n   830\t       request interception).\n   831\t\n   832\t       Why this matters (External Item 1 reasoning):\n   833\t       - Without centralization, each test redefines the shape\n   834\t         assertion, creating drift surface area.\n   835\t       - Adding a field to a response is one update in\n   836\t         _contract.py, automatically validated everywhere.\n   837\t       - This is NOT the same as Pydantic / OpenAPI \u2014 it's plain\n   838\t         Python assertions with no DSL or runtime validator. The\n   839\t         \u00a7 0.3 (g) pushback against schema DSLs still stands.\n   840\t\n   841\t       Naming convention: `assert_<endpoint>_response()` for\n   842\t       success shapes, `assert_<error_class>_error()` for error\n   843\t       shapes.\n   844\t\n   845\t       Plain functions, easy to read, no framework. Live alongside\n   846\t       the tests, not in production code.\n   847\t\n   848\t\u00a7 2.8  P43 \u2014 Trace_id decoupling: server-generated is canonical;\n   849\t       client-supplied is correlation hint only (NEW round 2).\n   850\t\n   851\t       External round-2 Item 2 surfaced that user-controlled URL\n   852\t       trace_ids corrupt log correlation:\n   853\t       - Multiple page-loads of the same URL \u2192 same client trace\n   854\t         under different server requests.\n   855\t       - Copy/paste URL forwarding \u2192 same trace under different\n   856\t         users.\n   857\t       - URL editing \u2192 arbitrary trace_id submitted.\n   858\t       - Refresh \u2192 new request, same client trace_id.\n   859\t\n   860\t       Mechanism (modifies S7b _resolve_external_trace_id per\n   861\t       \u00a7 1.2 (ii) carve-out):\n   862\t\n   863\t       Every C3a handler call ALWAYS mints a fresh\n   864\t       `server_trace_id` locally (16-hex). When inbound headers\n   865\t       contain a valid X-Trace-Id (matches /^[0-9a-f]{16}$/),\n   866\t       it is captured as `client_trace_id` for correlation but\n   867\t       is NOT used as the canonical trace.\n   868\t\n   869\t       Logging:\n   870\t       - Structured log lines include BOTH:\n   871\t         `server_trace_id=<canonical>`\n   872\t         `client_trace_id=<from_header_or_null>`\n   873\t       - Metric `c3a.endpoint` lines emit server_trace_id as\n   874\t         `trace_id` (preserving S7b \u00a7 5.2 schema).\n   875\t       - Operations / debugging correlate via server_trace_id.\n   876\t\n   877\t       **Per-surface mapping table (round 3 R3.1):**\n   878\t\n   879\t       | Surface                          | Trace_id field used |\n   880\t       |----------------------------------|---------------------|\n   881\t       | Response body `trace_id` field   | client_trace_id     |\n   882\t       | Metric `c3a.endpoint` line       | server_trace_id     |\n   883\t       | Structured request log lines     | BOTH (separate fields) |\n   884\t       | UI error display                 | client_trace_id     |\n   885\t       | Server-side error logs           | server_trace_id     |\n   886\t       | X-Trace-Id response header       | server_trace_id     |\n   887\t\n   888\t       Rationale: client trace = \"what user sees and reports\n   889\t       to support\"; server trace = \"canonical ID for log search.\"\n   890\t       Support workflow: user provides client_trace_id from UI;\n   891\t       support searches structured logs (which have BOTH fields)\n   892\t       to map client \u2192 server, then debugs via server_trace_id.\n   893\t\n   894\t       UI display:\n   895\t       - Error UI surfaces client_trace_id (the URL-embedded\n   896\t         one) for support narratives \u2014 \"share this code with\n   897\t         support.\"\n   898\t       - Support uses client_trace_id to look up server logs;\n   899\t         the join is on the structured log lines that record\n   900\t         both.\n   901\t\n   902\t       Test coverage: extends existing\n   903\t       test_c3a_session7b_trace_propagation.py tests; new tests\n   904\t       in test_c3a_status.py + test_c3a_state_machine.py\n   905\t       verify both fields appear in metric lines.\n   906\t\n   907\t       Why this matters: log correlation is foundational\n   908\t       observability. Without P43, an attacker editing trace_ids\n   909\t       in URLs could inject false correlation IDs into our\n   910\t       logs (log injection attack).\n   911\t\n   912\t================================================================\n[3245 lines total]",

INVARIANTS (NON-NEGOTIABLE)\n================================================================\n\nS8 introduces six new invariants (P36\u2013P41) that extend the S7b\nchain. None modify S7b's P19\u2013P35.\n\n\u00a7 2.1  P36 \u2014 Validation tests are deterministic (target).\n\n       Every test in `tests/validation/` and `tests/e2e/` aims\n       to produce the same result on every run, in every order,\n       on every machine running Python 3.10+ with the project's\n       pinned deps. **\"Zero flakes\" is the aspiration; <0.5%\n       flake rate measured over 100+ runs is the operational\n       target.** This reflects industry norms \u2014 Slack, GitHub,\n       and Atlassian (per TestDino benchmarks) target <2% with\n       retry-once policy; <0.5% is more strict and achievable\n       given S8's hermetic design.\n\n       Mechanism:\n       - Time is controlled via a `FrozenClock` fixture that\n         makes time.time() and int(time.time()) return\n         deterministic values.\n       - Random-token generators are seeded for the test session.\n       - SQLite uses `:memory:` or `tempfile` (no shared state\n         between tests).\n       - HTTP responses don't depend on real network (Resend\n         hook is mocked via `unittest.mock.patch` per S7b \u00a7 8.6).\n       - E2E tests use only Playwright `page.get_by_*` semantic\n         locators (which auto-wait per Playwright docs), NEVER\n         `waitForTimeout()` (Playwright docs explicitly call this\n         out as \"inherently flaky\"). Web-verified at multiple\n         sources (TestDino, BrowserStack, BetterStack, Semaphore).\n       - `C3A_TEST_MODE=1` env var disables the auto-retry logic\n         in _shared.js c3aFetch (External Item 3) \u2014 auto-retry\n         timing is itself a flake source.\n\n       Quarantine policy (per Slack / Atlassian pattern):\n       - A test that flakes \u22653 times in CI within 1 sprint is\n         tagged `@pytest.mark.flaky_quarantine` and assigned an\n         owner with a 1-sprint deadline to fix or delete.\n       - CI metrics track flake rate per test; >0.5% rate\n         triggers quarantine review.\n       - A retry-once policy is allowed in CI to prevent flakes\n         from blocking unrelated PRs; the retry is recorded so\n         flake rate metrics see it.\n\n       Verification: re-run the suite 10+ times in random order\n       during pre-merge testing; 0 flakes locally is the bar.\n       In CI: <0.5% over rolling 100 runs.\n\n\u00a7 2.2  P37 \u2014 Validation tests run in tiered time budgets.\n\n       Two tiers with separate hard caps (External Item 4):\n\n       **Tier 1 \u2014 `tests/validation/`** (in-process API tests):\n       - **30-second hard cap** on a developer laptop.\n       - Runs ALWAYS \u2014 every PR, every CI run, every local\n         `pytest`.\n       - Soft warning at 25s with **enforcement teeth (post-LOCK\n         PL2):** if Tier 1 exceeds 25s on **3 consecutive CI\n         runs**, this triggers a test-budget-review per the\n         hygiene rule in \u00a7 4.0.1 (audit recent test additions\n         for invariant-justification, consider pruning or\n         moving to Tier 2). Single-run >25s is a soft warning\n         only; sustained >25s is the trigger. CI implementation:\n         CI metric `c3a.tier1.duration_secs` tracked; alert\n         fires when 3 most-recent runs all >25s. Without this\n         enforcement teeth, the v0.3 \"soft warning\" was purely\n         advisory and would erode silently as tests accumulate.\n\n       **Tier 2 \u2014 `tests/e2e/`** (browser-driven Playwright tests):\n       - **60-second hard cap** on a developer laptop (includes\n         browser warmup ~5s).\n       - Tagged `@pytest.mark.e2e` \u2014 gated, NOT run by default\n         on PR pre-merge runs.\n       - Run on nightly CI + on pre-deploy verification runs.\n       - Locally invoked: `pytest -m e2e` or\n         `pytest tests/e2e/`.\n\n       **Combined cap when both run: 90 seconds.** Headroom\n       built into each tier independently so one tier blowing\n       its budget doesn't take down the other.\n\n       Current Tier 1 budget allocation post-PL1 + P-1\n       (concurrency 50\u219225, observability moved to Tier-2):\n       - state_machine ~5s (incl. 3 raw cross-check tests)\n       - idempotency ~3s\n       - termination ~3s\n       - 24h_fallback ~5s\n       - admin_recovery ~2s\n       - failure_modes ~2s\n       - concurrency ~2.6s (75 looped + 3 deterministic\n         worst-case anchor tests; v1.2 P-3)\n       - status ~1s\n       - validation fixture init/teardown overhead ~3s\n       - Total: ~26.6s; effective slack ~3.4s before 30s cap.\n       - **Target: ~27s steady-state with 3s buffer** (v1.2 P-1\n         hardening \u2014 was ~29.5s with ~0.5s slack in v1.1).\n\n       Tier 2 budget allocation (post-P-1 promotion +\n       v1.2 P-4 flow controller test):\n       - browser warmup (chromium launch) ~5s\n       - 5 browser flow tests \u00d7 ~7s ~35s\n       - 5 a11y tests \u00d7 ~3s ~15s\n       - observability tests promoted v1.2 P-1 (~6 tests) ~3s\n       - flow controller tests v1.2 P-4 (~3 tests) ~2s\n       - fixture overhead ~5s\n       - Total: ~65s; over the 60s soft cap.\n       - **Tier-2 cap raised v1.2: 75s** (was 60s) to\n         accommodate P-1 + P-4 additions. Acceptable because\n         Tier-2 only runs nightly + pre-deploy, never on PR\n         critical path.\n\n       **Tight-margin acknowledgment (v1.1 Item 2 + v1.2 P-1):**\n       v1.1's ~29.5s/30s budget was uncomfortably tight by\n       design. v1.2 P-1 hardens this by moving observability\n       (~3s; 6 tests, integration-test territory not core\n       correctness) from Tier 1 to Tier 2. New Tier 1 target\n       ~27s steady-state with 3s buffer. The previous safety\n       net remains:\n       (1) PL2 enforcement (\u00a7 2.2 above) \u2014 3-consecutive-runs\n           >25s triggers test-budget-review.\n       (2) \u00a7 4.0.1 hygiene rule \u2014 every new test justifies\n           an invariant; consolidation review on overlap.\n       (3) B-055 backlog item \u2014 captures post-launch tuning\n           trigger if real CI metrics show pressure.\n       Code build (Session 27+) MUST use the per-test budget\n       above as a hard constraint when implementing; if any\n       test exceeds its allocated slice during build, that\n       triggers review BEFORE adding the test, not after.\n\n       **PUSHBACK on prescriptive budget (v1.2 Item 1):** the\n       round-3 critic's \"Tier-1 must target ~24-26s\" rule is\n       prescription without CI variance data. Our actual CI\n       variance is unknown until S8 code ships. ~27s with 3s\n       buffer is more honest target \u2014 gives PL2 headroom AND\n       acknowledges the real budget rather than chasing a\n       lower number for its own sake. Post-launch B-055 trigger\n       will retune if real data shows the buffer is insufficient.\n\n       PR/CI gating:\n       - PR run: `pytest -m \"not e2e\"` (Tier 1 only \u2014 fast\n         feedback, gates merge)\n       - Nightly run: `pytest` (both tiers)\n       - Pre-deploy: `pytest` + `smoke_c3a_deploy.py` (full\n         in-process + post-deploy)\n\n       If P37 is at risk (3-consecutive-runs trigger fires),\n       address by:\n       - Sharing a single in-process HTTP server across tests\n         in the same module (setUpClass / setUpModule / pytest\n         fixture session scope).\n       - Using `:memory:` SQLite where the test doesn't need\n         persistence across in-process actors.\n       - Moving lower-priority tests from Tier 1 to Tier 2 via\n         `@pytest.mark.e2e` retag.\n       - Reusing the playwright browser context across e2e tests\n         in the same module.\n       - NOT by parallelizing test execution. Determinism (P36)\n         > speed.\n\n\u00a7 2.3  P38 \u2014 Validation tests are HERMETIC.\n\n       Test execution does not require:\n       - Network access\n       - A running Resend account\n       - A specific machine clock value\n       - Environment variables beyond what the test sets itself\n         (in particular: NO RESEND_API_KEY required; NO\n         BUILDEMUP_DATABASE_PATH required)\n       - File-system locations outside tempfile.gettempdir()\n\n       Playwright e2e tests require the chromium binary\n       (one-time `playwright install chromium` per dev machine\n       and per CI runner) but the test execution itself does not\n       need network beyond localhost.\n\n       Hermetic = reproducible = can run anywhere.\n\n\u00a7 2.4  P39 \u2014 Validation tests document the C3a contract.\n\n       Each test class has a docstring naming the invariant or\n       behavior under test in spec terms. A reader who finds a\n       test failure can identify which P-invariant or which \u00a7\n       section is being exercised without reading the test body.\n\n       Operational benefit: when production behavior drifts from\n       spec, the failing test names which contract is being\n       violated.\n\n\u00a7 2.5  P40 \u2014 UI tests exercise UI through a real browser engine.\n\n       E2E tests use Playwright + chromium against a live\n       in-process server. They do NOT use jsdom, JSDOMs, or any\n       DOM-mock library. The browser engine catches real-world\n       issues (layout, focus management, form submission encoding,\n       redirect handling) that mocks cannot.\n\n       Cost: one-time `playwright install chromium` per\n       dev/CI machine. Worth it for the realism guarantee.\n\n\u00a7 2.6  P41 \u2014 UI tests use semantic selectors.\n\n       Selectors prefer (in order):\n       (a) ARIA role + accessible name: `page.get_by_role(\"button\",\n           name=\"Confirm\")`\n       (b) Form label: `page.get_by_label(\"Email\")`\n       (c) Visible text: `page.get_by_text(\"Resolve case\")`\n       (d) test-id attributes (data-testid=\"...\") for genuinely\n           unique non-textual elements.\n\n       Selectors do NOT use:\n       - Brittle CSS path selectors (`.container > .card:nth-child(2)`)\n       - Auto-generated class names\n       - XPath in any form\n\n       Tests survive cosmetic CSS changes by depending on\n       semantics, not layout.\n\n\u00a7 2.7  P42 \u2014 UI\u2013API contract is centralized (External Item 1).\n\n       All response-shape assertions live in\n       `tests/validation/_contract.py` as plain Python functions:\n\n       ```python\n       def assert_check_response(resp: dict) -> None:\n           \"\"\"Asserts /api/extreme-case/check 200 response shape.\"\"\"\n           assert \"session_token\" in resp\n           assert isinstance(resp[\"session_token\"], str)\n           assert resp.get(\"ok\") is True\n           assert \"trace_id\" in resp\n           assert _TRACE_ID_RE.match(resp[\"trace_id\"])\n           # ... full shape check including extreme_case nested fields\n       ```\n\n       One assertion function per endpoint:\n       - assert_check_response()\n       - assert_resolve_response()\n       - assert_abort_response()\n       - assert_cba_verified_response()\n       - assert_status_response()       (new in v0.2)\n       - assert_validation_error()       (4xx shape)\n       - assert_storage_busy_error()     (503 shape)\n\n       Both validation tests AND e2e tests use these \u2014 UI tests\n       can call assert_check_response() against the JSON they\n       observe in the browser network tab (via Playwright's\n       request interception).\n\n       Why this matters (External Item 1 reasoning):\n       - Without centralization, each test redefines the shape\n         assertion, creating drift surface area.\n       - Adding a field to a response is one update in\n         _contract.py, automatically validated everywhere.\n       - This is NOT the same as Pydantic / OpenAPI \u2014 it's plain\n         Python assertions with no DSL or runtime validator. The\n         \u00a7 0.3 (g) pushback against schema DSLs still stands.\n\n       Naming convention: `assert_<endpoint>_response()` for\n       success shapes, `assert_<error_class>_error()` for error\n       shapes.\n\n       Plain functions, easy to read, no framework. Live alongside\n       the tests, not in production code.\n\n\u00a7 2.8  P43 \u2014 Trace_id decoupling: server-generated is canonical;\n       client-supplied is correlation hint only (NEW round 2).\n\n       External round-2 Item 2 surfaced that user-controlled URL\n       trace_ids corrupt log correlation:\n       - Multiple page-loads of the same URL \u2192 same client trace\n         under different server requests.\n       - Copy/paste URL forwarding \u2192 same trace under different\n         users.\n       - URL editing \u2192 arbitrary trace_id submitted.\n       - Refresh \u2192 new request, same client trace_id.\n\n       Mechanism (modifies S7b _resolve_external_trace_id per\n       \u00a7 1.2 (ii) carve-out):\n\n       Every C3a handler call ALWAYS mints a fresh\n       `server_trace_id` locally (16-hex). When inbound headers\n       contain a valid X-Trace-Id (matches /^[0-9a-f]{16}$/),\n       it is captured as `client_trace_id` for correlation but\n       is NOT used as the canonical trace.\n\n       Logging:\n       - Structured log lines include BOTH:\n         `server_trace_id=<canonical>`\n         `client_trace_id=<from_header_or_null>`\n       - Metric `c3a.endpoint` lines emit server_trace_id as\n         `trace_id` (preserving S7b \u00a7 5.2 schema).\n       - Operations / debugging correlate via server_trace_id.\n\n       **Per-surface mapping table (round 3 R3.1):**\n\n       | Surface                          | Trace_id field used |\n       |----------------------------------|---------------------|\n       | Response body `trace_id` field   | client_trace_id     |\n       | Metric `c3a.endpoint` line       | server_trace_id     |\n       | Structured request log lines     | BOTH (separate fields) |\n       | UI error display                 | client_trace_id     |\n       | Server-side error logs           | server_trace_id     |\n       | X-Trace-Id response header       | server_trace_id     |\n\n       Rationale: client trace = \"what user sees and reports\n       to support\"; server trace = \"canonical ID for log search.\"\n       Support workflow: user provides client_trace_id from UI;\n       support searches structured logs (which have BOTH fields)\n       to map client \u2192 server, then debugs via server_trace_id.\n\n       UI display:\n       - Error UI surfaces client_trace_id (the URL-embedded\n         one) for support narratives \u2014 \"share this code with\n         support.\"\n       - Support uses client_trace_id to look up server logs;\n         the join is on the structured log lines that record\n         both.\n\n       Test coverage: extends existing\n       test_c3a_session7b_trace_propagation.py tests; new tests\n       in test_c3a_status.py + test_c3a_state_machine.py\n       verify both fields appear in metric lines.\n\n       Why this matters: log correlation is foundational\n       observability. Without P43, an attacker editing trace_ids\n       in URLs could inject false correlation IDs into our\n       logs (log injection attack).\n\n================================================================\n",

                            trace_id per P43\n                                            (round 2 X2)\n       ```\n\n\u00a7 3.2  Fixture architecture (validation suite).\n\n       Tests use a layered fixture model:\n\n       (a) `temp_db_path` (function-scoped) \u2014 produces a unique\n           SQLite path under tempfile.gettempdir(). Cleanup on\n           teardown.\n\n       (b) `fresh_storage(temp_db_path)` \u2014 initializes\n           GateStateStorage and SchedulerStateStorage against\n           that path, with `_ensure_schema` applied. Returns a\n           tuple. Resets the singleton injection points in the\n           api modules so tests don't leak storage between runs.\n\n       (c) `frozen_clock(t0=...)` \u2014 patches time.time and\n           datetime.utcnow to return controllable values. Tests\n           manipulate the clock with `frozen_clock.advance(secs)`\n           rather than time.sleep. Required for the 24h fallback\n           test.\n\n       (d) `live_server(fresh_storage, frozen_clock)` \u2014 spins up\n           an in-process HTTPServer thread on a free port. Yields\n           a base URL. Teardown does srv.shutdown() + thread.join.\n           Module-scoped where individual tests don't need\n           isolation, function-scoped where they do.\n\n       (e) `mocked_resend(live_server)` \u2014 patches urllib.request\n           so the email hook records calls without sending.\n\n       (f) `seeded_brief(fresh_storage, brief_token=..., ...)` \u2014\n           inserts a `gate_states` row representing a previously-\n           captured brief, so c3a tests start from a valid\n           C1-output state without coupling to C1. **Uses\n           `GateStateStorage.save()` (the public API) NOT raw\n           SQL inserts** (Finding 6) \u2014 decouples S8 from the\n           gate_states schema. If the schema evolves, only\n           GateStateStorage.save() needs updating, not every\n           S8 fixture call site.\n\n       Fixtures live in `tests/validation/conftest.py`. pytest's\n       fixture model is the host (we already use pytest project-\n       wide).\n\n\u00a7 3.3  Fixture architecture (e2e suite).\n\n       E2E tests reuse the validation fixtures plus playwright-\n       specific ones:\n\n       (g) `browser` (session-scoped) \u2014 playwright Browser\n           instance. One per test session. Headless by default;\n           `PLAYWRIGHT_HEADLESS=false` for local debugging.\n\n       (h) `context(browser)` (function-scoped) \u2014 fresh\n           BrowserContext per test (cookies, storage isolated\n           per test).\n\n       (i) `page(context)` (function-scoped) \u2014 Playwright Page.\n\n       (j) `c3a_test_environment` \u2014 wraps fresh_storage +\n           live_server + mocked_resend + frozen_clock + browser\n           into one fixture for e2e tests so each test gets the\n           full stack.\n\n       Fixtures live in `tests/e2e/conftest.py`.\n\n\u00a7 3.4  Test seams (what's mocked vs real):\n\n       REAL:\n       - SchedulerStateStorage and GateStateStorage (against temp\n         SQLite) \u2014 these ARE under test.\n       - All five c3a_endpoint handlers \u2014 under test.\n       - admin_endpoint, health_endpoint, server.py \u2014 under test.\n       - JSON parsing, HTTP framing, content-length, CORS headers.\n       - Static file serving (under test for the new c3a routes).\n       - Playwright's chromium browser (real DOM, real CSS, real JS).\n\n       MOCKED:\n       - Resend email hook (urllib.request.urlopen). Test\n         captures the constructed Request to assert on shape;\n         the hook's action is recorded but no network call.\n       - `time.time()` and `datetime.utcnow()` in tests that\n         exercise time-window logic.\n\n       NEITHER REAL NOR MOCKED (i.e., not exercised at all):\n       - C1 (brief capture). Tests seed brief_token rows into\n         gate_states directly via fixture.\n       - C2 (feasibility). Out of S8 scope.\n       - The Resend service. Hook is mocked; nothing reaches\n         the real Resend.\n\n\u00a7 3.5  Test data strategy.\n\n       (a) Brief tokens are deterministic UUIDs based on test\n           name (e.g., `brief-test_happy_path-001`) so failures\n           are debuggable.\n       (b) Session tokens come from the real handler responses\n           (not synthesized) so the format-version chain stays\n           realistic.\n       (c) Fixture brief_token data uses minimal valid JSON\n           shapes \u2014 just enough to satisfy gate_state validators.\n       (d) No real PII in test data. Email addresses are of the\n           form `test+<test_name>@example.test` (a TLD reserved\n           by RFC 2606 for testing).\n\n================================================================\n\u00a7 4. TEST PLAN \u2014 VALIDATION SUITE\n================================================================\n\n\u00a7 4.0  Centralized contract module + cross-check layer.\n\n",

User reopens an old tab after the scheduler fired the\n         fallback.\n       - User clicks an email link 26 hours later (case has\n         already been resolved).\n       - Admin reset cleared a case the user thought was active.\n\n\u00a7 5a.2 Request.\n\n       ```\n       GET /api/extreme-case/status?session_token=<token>\n       Headers:\n         X-Trace-Id: <16-hex>  (optional, propagated)\n       ```\n\n\u00a7 5a.3 Response.\n\n       Success (200):\n       ```json\n       {\n         \"ok\": true,\n         \"session_token\": \"<token>\",\n         \"current_state\": \"CASE_PENDING\",\n         \"terminal_state\": null,\n         \"is_terminal\": false,\n         \"last_updated_at\": 1730509200,\n         \"trace_id\": \"0123456789abcdef\"\n       }\n       ```\n\n       Or for terminal session:\n       ```json\n       {\n         \"ok\": true,\n         \"session_token\": \"<token>\",\n         \"current_state\": \"CBA_VERIFIED\",\n         \"terminal_state\": \"CBA_VERIFIED\",\n         \"is_terminal\": true,\n         \"last_updated_at\": 1730599200,\n         \"trace_id\": \"0123456789abcdef\"\n       }\n       ```\n\n       Errors:\n       - 400 validation: missing session_token query param\n       - 404 unknown_token: session_token not found in\n         gate_states (purged by TTL or never existed)\n\n\u00a7 5a.4 Implementation outline.\n\n       New file `api/c3a_status_endpoint.py` (~80 LOC).\n\n       ```python\n       def handle_status(query_params: dict) -> tuple[int, dict]:\n           trace_id = _new_trace_id()  # mint locally per \u00a7 5.4\n           token = query_params.get('session_token')\n           if not token:\n               return 400, {\n                   'ok': False,\n                   'errors': [{\n                       'code': 'validation',\n                       'message': 'session_token required'\n                   }],\n                   'trace_id': trace_id,\n               }\n           row = _gate_state_storage().load(token)\n           if row is None:\n               return 404, {\n                   'ok': False,\n                   'errors': [{\n                       'code': 'unknown_token',\n                       'message': 'session not found'\n                   }],\n                   'trace_id': trace_id,\n               }\n           state = json.loads(row.state_json)\n           return 200, {\n               'ok': True,\n               'session_token': token,\n               'current_state': state.get('current_state'),\n               'terminal_state': state.get('terminal_state'),\n               'is_terminal': bool(row.is_terminal),\n               'last_updated_at': row.saved_at,\n               'trace_id': trace_id,\n           }\n       ```\n\n       Wired into server.py as a GET route per \u00a7 1.2 (ii)\n       carve-out. Reuses GateStateStorage singleton (already\n       loaded by S7b).\n\n       **Read consistency note (round 2 X6):** /status reads\n       gate_states via GateStateStorage.load() which uses\n       _connect() \u2014 SQLite in WAL mode (per S7b \u00a7 7.1). Per\n       sqlite.org official docs, WAL mode provides \"snapshot\n       isolation\": when a read transaction starts, that reader\n       sees an unchanging snapshot of the database file as it\n       existed at that moment; concurrent writes are invisible\n       to the read. So /status responses are atomic snapshots\n       \u2014 partial-state reads (\"scheduler updates state, /status\n       reads mid-update\") are impossible at row granularity.\n       Additionally, gate_states.state_json is a single TEXT\n       column replaced atomically in writes, so even at the\n       conceptual level the proposed \"partial JSON\" race is\n       impossible.\n\n       (Round-2 X6 PUSH-BACK rationale: the proposed\n       transactional-snapshot wrappers / version field would\n       add code surface for a problem that doesn't exist\n       under SQLite WAL semantics. Web-verified via\n       sqlite.org/isolation.html.)\n\n\u00a7 5a.5 Authentication and threat model (round 2 X1).\n\n       Per Q12 (RESOLVED round 1, refined round 2): NO auth\n       required at endpoint level. session_token entropy is\n       the security boundary.\n\n       **Threat model \u2014 what session_token entropy protects against:**\n\n       - **Brute-force enumeration: PROTECTED.** session_token\n         is generated as a v4 UUID (122 bits of cryptographic\n         randomness). At 122 bits, brute-force is computationally\n         infeasible \u2014 current best supercomputers cannot\n         enumerate 2^122 values in human timescales. Web-verified\n         via Hacker News / RFC 4122 / cryptographic-token\n         design references.\n\n       **Threat model \u2014 what session_token entropy does NOT\n       protect against (round 2 X1 valid concerns):**\n\n       - **Token leakage via referrer headers** \u2014 possible if\n         the user clicks a link from /c3a/case.html to an\n         external site; the URL with session_token leaks via\n         Referer header. Mitigation: meta tag\n         `<meta name=\"referrer\" content=\"no-referrer-when-downgrade\">`\n         in all C3a pages (already standard practice). Add to\n         \u00a7 5.7 _shared.css (or HTML <head> templates).\n\n       - **Token leakage via browser history** \u2014 possible if\n         the user shares a screenshot of their browser, or\n         someone has physical access to the machine. NOT\n         mitigatable at the URL level (URLs are user-visible\n         by design). Operationally addressed by short\n         gate_states TTL (currently 24h+grace per S7b config).\n\n       - **Token leakage via email link forwarding** \u2014 real\n         concern. User receives cba-checklist email, forwards\n         it to a colleague \u2014 now colleague has the token.\n         Mitigation options (NOT in S8 scope):\n         (a) Short-lived \"view tokens\" derived from session\n             token + email-send-time HMAC \u2014 proper auth-system\n             work, in B-042.\n         (b) Email warning text \u2014 \"this link is single-use,\n             do not forward\" (UX, not security; but cheap).\n             Add to c3a_email_hook email body content as a\n             round-2 PATCH-IN-S8-SCOPE.\n\n       - **Token leakage via server logs** \u2014 gate_states writes\n         and request log lines may contain session_token. S7b\n         instrumentation per \u00a7 5.2 explicitly does NOT log\n         session_token in metric lines (closed P30 vocabulary\n         includes only handler/status/duration/trace_id/error_class).\n         If application logs elsewhere capture session_token,\n         that's an existing concern documented in B-053 (log\n         shipping with retention).\n\n       **Symmetry with existing endpoints:** /resolve, /abort,\n       /cba-verified are all session_token-keyed and unauthed.\n       /status doesn't increase the leakage surface \u2014 same\n       threat applies to all four. Closing /status alone\n       without the others is band-aid; closing all four\n       together is the proper auth-system work, B-042.\n\n       **Round-2 X1 PUSH-BACK rationale:**\n\n       - On Option A (status_token alongside session_token):\n         adding a second token + storage column + lifecycle\n         logic creates inconsistency unless ALSO applied to\n         /resolve, /abort, /cba-verified \u2014 i.e., it's the\n         B-042 work scoped wrong.\n\n       - On Option B (rate limiting): proper rate limiting is\n         middleware work; applying it to /status alone is band-\n         aid. Goes to B-042.\n\n       - On Option C (token format prefix/version/hash): adds\n         zero security at 122 bits of entropy; cargo-cult\n         security theater.\n\n       v1.0 LOCKED actions:\n       - This documentation block (\u00a7 5a.5) makes the threat\n         model explicit so future readers don't re-raise.\n       - PATCH-IN-S8-SCOPE: c3a_email_hook adds \"do not forward\"\n         note in email body. **Round 3 R3.6 relabel: this is a\n         UX hint to set user expectation, NOT security\n         mitigation.** Real protection against email forwarding\n         requires short-lived view tokens (B-042). Users\n         routinely forward emails despite warnings; the warning\n         doesn't change adversary capability.\n       - Referrer meta tag added to all C3a pages with the\n         strictest setting (round 3 R3.7): `<meta name=\"referrer\"\n         content=\"no-referrer\">`. Blocks ALL Referer-header\n         leakage of session_token, not just downgrade scenarios.\n         (`no-referrer-when-downgrade` was browser default in\n         modern Firefox/Chrome and added zero value.)\n       - Proper rate limiting / auth: deferred to B-042\n         (existing backlog).\n\n================================================================\n\u00a7 5b   The /api/extreme-case/check-init endpoint\n",

UAs matching any\n       of the following patterns (case-insensitive substring\n       match) get the bot response:\n\n       ```python\n       BOT_UA_PATTERNS = (\n           # Link preview crawlers (web-verified)\n           \"facebookexternalhit\",   # Meta link previews\n           \"facebot\",\n           \"slackbot\",              # Slack link unfurl\n           \"twitterbot\",            # Twitter/X cards\n           \"linkedinbot\",           # LinkedIn previews\n           \"telegrambot\",           # Telegram previews\n           \"whatsapp\",              # WhatsApp previews\n           \"discordbot\",            # Discord embeds\n           \"blueskypreviewbot\",     # Bluesky previews\n           # Prefetch / privacy proxies\n           \"chrome-lighthouse\",     # Lighthouse audits\n           \"googlebot\",             # Google crawler\n           \"bingbot\",               # Bing crawler\n           \"duckduckbot\",           # DuckDuckGo\n           # AI training / general crawlers\n           \"anthropic-ai\",\n           \"claudebot\",\n           \"gptbot\",\n           \"ccbot\",                 # Common Crawl\n           \"bytespider\",            # ByteDance\n           # Generic patterns (last-resort)\n           \"bot\",\n           \"crawler\",\n           \"spider\",\n           \"scraper\",\n       )\n\n       def _is_bot_ua(ua: str) -> bool:\n           if not ua:\n               return True  # Missing UA treated as suspicious\n           ua_low = ua.lower()\n           return any(pat in ua_low for pat in BOT_UA_PATTERNS)\n       ```\n\n       Notes:\n       - Generic patterns (\"bot\", \"crawler\") last in the list\n         catch unknown variants. Some legitimate user agents\n         contain \"bot\" (e.g., \"Robot Turtle browser\" \u2014 extremely\n         rare); accepted as false-positive cost.\n       - This is bot-MITIGATION, NOT security (bots can spoof\n         UAs). Proper protection against malicious UA-spoofing\n         is the auth layer in B-042.\n       - The list is MAINTAINED in code; new bot patterns can\n         be added without spec amendment (additive to the\n         constant tuple).\n\n\u00a7 5b.6 Test coverage.\n\n       test_c3a_status.py extends to cover (v1.2 P-2):\n       - GET /check-init with legitimate UA \u2192 200 + session\n         created (existing behavior).\n       - GET /check-init with User-Agent: facebookexternalhit\n         \u2192 200 + preview HTML + NO session in DB.\n       - GET /check-init with User-Agent: Slackbot-LinkExpanding\n         \u2192 200 + preview HTML + NO session.\n       - GET /check-init with empty User-Agent \u2192 200 + preview\n         HTML (suspicious-default behavior).\n       - GET /check-init with browser UA \u2192 session created\n         normally.\n\n       +5 tests in test_c3a_status.py (was 7, now 12).\n\n\u00a7 5b.7 Implementation outline.\n\n       ```python\n       # api/c3a_check_init_endpoint.py (extended v1.2 P-2)\n       def handle_check_init(query_params, headers):\n           ua = headers.get('User-Agent', '')\n           if _is_bot_ua(ua):\n               return 200, {\n                   '_html_response': True,\n                   'body': BOT_PREVIEW_HTML,\n                   'extra_headers': {\n                       'Content-Type': 'text/html; charset=utf-8',\n                       'Cache-Control': 'no-store, no-cache, must-revalidate',\n                   },\n               }\n           # Legitimate UA \u2014 call existing POST handler\n           brief_token = query_params.get('brief')\n           request_id = query_params.get('request_id')\n           # Validate per S7a /check contract\n           ...\n           # Dispatch to inner _handle_check\n           return _handle_check_inner(brief_token, request_id, headers)\n       ```\n\n       server.py routes /check-init GET to this handler.\n\n================================================================\n\u00a7 6. CONTRACT DOCUMENT \u2014 UI \u2190\u2192 API\n================================================================\n\n\u00a7 6.1  Endpoints C3a UI consumes.\n\n       POST /api/extreme-case/check\n       POST /api/extreme-case/resolve\n       POST /api/extreme-case/abort\n       POST /api/extreme-case/cba-verified\n\n       Plus, NOT consumed by UI but mentioned for completeness:\n       POST /api/extreme-case/cba-fallback-continue\n            (USED BY THE SCHEDULER)\n\n\u00a7 6.2  Request/response shapes.\n\n       Each endpoint's shape is fully specified in the\n       c3a_endpoint module docstrings + S7a SPEC \u00a7 5. The UI\n       contract is: those shapes are the source of truth and\n       won't change without a new C3a sub-session spec.\n\n       Headers the UI MUST send:\n       - Content-Type: application/json on POSTs.\n       - X-Trace-Id: <16-hex> (always, via _shared.js\n         c3aFetch helper).\n\n       Headers the UI MUST handle:\n       - Retry-After (on 503 storage_busy responses).\n       - Standard CORS headers (Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *\n         is set by server.py).\n\n\u00a7 6.3  Status code semantics for UI consumers:\n\n       200 \u2014 success. Body always JSON object with `ok: true`.\n       400 \u2014 validation. Body has `errors: [{code, message}]`.\n       401 \u2014 auth. Admin endpoints only; UI shouldn't see this.\n       404 \u2014 unknown_token. Show \"session not found\" UX.\n       409 \u2014 already_fired (admin reset only).\n       410 \u2014 terminal. Show \"this case is closed\" UX.\n       503 \u2014 storage_busy. Honor Retry-After; show transient\n             error UX via _shared.js c3aFetch helper.\n\n\u00a7 6.4  Trace_id propagation expectation.\n\n       UI generates a 16-hex trace_id at the start of a user\n       session (via _shared.js traceId()), sends it on every\n       C3a call, and surfaces it in any error UI (\"if you\n       contact support, share this code: <trace_id>\").\n\n================================================================\n\u00a7 7. REQUEST-RESPONSE FLOWS (UI flows)\n================================================================\n",
